# HTTP Core

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document Type | scope_srs |
| Scope | HTTP |
| Scope Name | HTTP Core |

## 1. Scope Overview

This scope covers the HTTP core of NaruServe (issue #4 plus route registration and dispatch from #2 and #3, wave 3): the public `Request`, `Response`, `RouteHandler`, `RouteBuilder` and `RestApp` interfaces with the methods whose types exist by the end of wave 3, the engine-free dispatcher that maps router lookup results to handler calls or automatic responses, request accessors (path, raw path, parameters, query, headers, attributes), request body ownership (`BodyState`, `BodyAlreadyConsumedException`, limit-enforcing input stream), the response API and its state machine, the `HttpAdapter` SPI with an in-memory adapter in the `NaruServe-api` test fixtures, and the `docs/api` markers decided in wave 3. It holds the HTTP-level assertions of T01, T02, T03, T05 and T06 and the in-memory part of T07.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- `Request`, `Response`, `RouteHandler`, `RouteBuilder`, `RestApp` and `BodyAlreadyConsumedException` in `NaruServe-api` under package `com.snoworca.naruserve`, and the internal `RestApp` implementation, dispatcher, `BodyState` and limit-enforcing stream in an internal package.
- The `HttpAdapter` SPI and its extension points, and the in-memory adapter plus test harness in the `NaruServe-api` test fixtures source set.
- The (미확정) markers owned by #4 and D-398 in `docs/api/request.md`, `docs/api/response.md`, `docs/api/routing.md`, `docs/api/app.md`, `docs/api/defaults.md` and `docs/api/express-migration.md`.

### Out of Scope

- `req.service`, `req.application` (wave 4); `req.body`, `res.json` and the cookie methods (wave 5); `req.form`, `req.file`, `req.files`, `req.parts`, `res.file`, `res.download`, `res.cache` (wave 6); `res.sse` (wave 7) (D-392).
- `listen()`, the lifecycle states, the public entry point `RestApp.create()` and the standard error body and error path (wave 4).
- The TinyHttpd halves of D-399, D-442 and I-021 (wave 5, `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/carried-into-wave-5.md`) and `426` over TinyHttpd (wave 7, `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/carried-into-wave-7.md`).
- HTTP-level `413` checks through body readers (waves 5 and 6).
- Any change inside the TinyHttpd checkout.

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- Core code is engine-free code in `NaruServe-api`: it references no TinyHttpd, Servlet, Gson, `android.*` or Kotlin stdlib type, compiles to Java 8 bytecode and passes the wave 1 animal-sniffer API 21 and class-reference checks.
- Public types follow the wave 1 conventions: nullability annotations on public signatures, `@FunctionalInterface` callbacks, Express-style short method names and no Kotlin keywords as method names.
- Where the design leaves a choice open, the wave decisions of `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-3/design.md` apply; otherwise the implementer decides under standing rule C-07, following RFC 9110 and, between equally valid options, Express behaviour.
- Source of the requirements: `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-3/design.md` (frozen design items, wave decisions, residual), `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-3/excerpt.md` and `object.md`.

## 4. Requirements

### IR-HTTP-001 — Request and Response public surface of wave 3

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-surface |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide public `Request` and `Response` interfaces in package `com.snoworca.naruserve` holding the `docs/api/request.md` and `docs/api/response.md` methods whose parameter and return types exist by the end of wave 3, excluding every method that D-392 assigns to a later wave, with every reference-type parameter and return value non-null through the package's `@NullMarked` annotation or annotated `@Nullable`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-071, D-072, D-392 and the wave decision on the request and response surface (D-392 overrides D-071). Supports T01, T02, T03, T05, T06 and T07.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `Request` is a public interface in `com.snoworca.naruserve` declaring `method()`, `path()`, `rawPath()`, `param(name)`, `query(name)`, `queries(name)`, `header(name)`, `headers(name)`, `remoteAddress()`, `isSecure()`, `lastEventId()`, `attribute(key, value)` and `attribute(key, type)`, with `method()` returning `Method`. [D-071] Decision: the write overload `attribute(String, @Nullable Object)` returns `void` and the read overload is generic `<T> @Nullable T attribute(String, Class<T>)`, so a literal `null` second argument resolves to the read overload. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: `Response` is a public interface in `com.snoworca.naruserve` declaring `status(code)`, `header(name, value)`, `send(String)`, `send(byte[])`, `html(String)`, `stream(InputStream, contentType)`, `end()`, `isCommitted()` and `abort(error)`. [D-071] Decision: `status` and `header` return the receiver; `send`, `html`, `stream`, `end` and `abort` return `void`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-3: At the wave 3 merge, a reflection test finds no method named `body`, `form`, `file`, `files`, `parts`, `json`, `download`, `cache`, `sse`, `cookie`, `getCookies`, `addCookie`, `service` or `application` on `Request` or `Response`, even where the parameter and return types of such a method exist in wave 3; the later waves of D-392 add them. [D-392; wave decision: Request and response surface]
- [x] AC-4: D-072 is met by the wave 1 convention: package `com.snoworca.naruserve` is annotated JSpecify `@NullMarked` (non-null by default, which counts as `@NonNull`) and every nullable reference-type parameter or return value of `Request` and `Response` carries `@Nullable`; a reflection test checks the package annotation and the `@Nullable` positions that `docs/api/request.md` and `docs/api/response.md` list as nullable (such as the returns of `query`, `header` and `attribute(key, type)`). [D-072] Decision: exactly these positions carry `@Nullable`: the returns of `query`, `header`, `lastEventId` and `attribute(key, type)`, and the parameters `value` of `attribute(key, value)` and `error` of `abort(error)`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-5: A Kotlin source test compiles and runs calls to the wave 3 `Request` and `Response` methods, assigning `req.query("q")` to `String?` and `req.path()` to `String` without platform types. [D-072] Decision: the Kotlin test routes a request through the test-fixtures `TestApp` harness and calls every wave 3 `Request` and `Response` method from a Kotlin lambda. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 SDS-AC-4]

### IR-HTTP-002 — RouteHandler functional interface

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-surface, routing |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide `RouteHandler` as a public `@FunctionalInterface` in `com.snoworca.naruserve` whose single abstract method is `void handle(Request req, Response res) throws Exception`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-045. Used by T01, T02, T03, T05 and T06.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A reflection test finds `RouteHandler` public, annotated `@FunctionalInterface` and declaring exactly one abstract method `handle(Request, Response)` with return type `void` and `throws Exception`. [D-045]
- [x] AC-2: A Java lambda that throws a checked `IOException` and a Kotlin lambda `{ req, res -> }` both compile as `RouteHandler` without wrapping. [D-045]

### IR-HTTP-003 — HttpAdapter SPI exchange data contract

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-spi |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall define an `HttpAdapter` SPI in `NaruServe-api` through which an adapter gives the core the method token, the raw request-target path, the raw query string, the headers, a streaming body `InputStream`, the remote address and the secure flag of each request, and through which the core sends status, headers and a streaming body `OutputStream`. The core decodes the raw path and raw query once; adapters never pass decoded values.

#### Rationale

Covers D-105, the SPI half of D-399 and the wave decision on adapter halves. The TinyHttpd half of D-399 (reading `getRequestURI()` and `getQueryString()`, never `getPath()`) and the adapter value comparison of the residual (`/a//b` under `mergeSlashes(true)`, `/a%20b` to `/:x`, compared by `rawPath()` and decoded values) are deferred to wave 5 (`docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/carried-into-wave-5.md`).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The SPI exposes, per request, the request line as the method token, the raw request-target path, the raw query string (or its absence) and the HTTP version, plus the header names and values in received order, a body `InputStream`, the remote address and the secure flag. [D-105] Decision: the headers are exposed as a `List<Map.Entry<String, String>>` in received order with duplicates kept, not as a `Map`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: The SPI accepts from the core a status code, response headers and a body `OutputStream`, and the core writes status and headers before the first body byte. [D-105] Decision: status, headers and the content length (`-1` when unknown) pass in one `commit` call that returns the body `OutputStream`, and a second start of the response throws `IllegalStateException`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-2] The exchange outlives `ExchangeHandler#handle` until the core calls `finish` or `abort`, and the adapter may force-close it only at its stop deadline; `abort()` is idempotent and thread-safe (it may run on any thread, also while another thread is in `commit`, a body write or `finish`, which then fail with an `IOException`). The headers passed to `commit` are sent as given: they already carry `Content-Length` when the core knows it (a `HEAD` response carries the length the `GET` body would have had) and the adapter never adds, removes or changes `Content-Length`; `contentLength` is the number of body bytes the core will write, `0` for a bodyless response (`204`, `304`, `HEAD`) or `-1` when unknown, which the adapter frames itself (chunked, or close on HTTP/1.0) and is the only case where it may add a framing header. Header names and values are ISO-8859-1: the core guarantees every char is at most U+00FF and never CR or LF, so the adapter writes each char as one byte. [wave 3 implementation decision]
- [x] AC-3: The raw request-target path and the raw query string travel through the SPI as separate undecoded fields: an exchange with target `/a%20b?x=%41` reaches the core with raw path `/a%20b` and raw query `x=%41`, and the core alone yields `req.path()` = `/a b` and `req.query("x")` = `A`. [D-399; wave decision: Adapter halves] Decision: the raw query is `null` when the target has no `?`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-3]

### IR-HTTP-004 — HttpAdapter SPI extension points and bind/stop lifecycle

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:medium, http-spi |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall include in the `HttpAdapter` SPI extension points for file transfer, event streams and protocol upgrade, and lifecycle calls that bind synchronously to an address and port, report the actual bound port, and stop in two calls: a blocking stop bounded by a core-supplied deadline and a final release bounded by a fixed margin.

#### Rationale

Covers D-106 and D-107. Waves 6, 7 and 8 add adapter classes against these points without changing the SPI. No concrete deadline or margin value is set here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Through the file-transfer extension point the core hands the adapter a `java.io.File` with an offset and a length, and the adapter writes exactly those bytes of the file as the response body; an in-memory test hands over a file with a non-zero offset and a length shorter than the rest of the file and reads back exactly that byte range from the client body. [D-106]
- [x] AC-2: Through the protocol-upgrade extension point the core hands the adapter an upgrade callback with a `101` response, and the adapter invokes that callback with the exchange's raw input and output streams after writing the `101` response; an in-memory test observes that the callback ran with the exchange's streams (or that the in-memory adapter recorded the hand-over). [D-106]
- [x] AC-3: The event-stream point hands the adapter a core-owned drain loop that keeps writing after the handler returned until the core reports the stream ended, and the adapter chooses the thread that runs it; in wave 3 (before `res.sse` exists) an in-memory test supplies a drain loop through the SPI type, runs it on an adapter-chosen thread after the handler returned and sees it end only when the end is reported. [D-106] Decision: `eventStream` returns to the caller at once, and the in-memory adapter runs the drain on its own worker thread, completing the client response when the drain returns. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-4: The three extension points are SPI types in `NaruServe-api`, usable by an adapter without any NaruServe class outside `NaruServe-api`, so waves 6, 7 and 8 add adapter classes against them without changing the SPI. [D-106]
- [x] AC-5: The SPI declares a bind to an address and port that returns only after binding completed, and a separate call through which the core reads the actual bound port; with the in-memory adapter, a bind with port `0` reports a positive bound port that the core reads back through the SPI and that stays the same on every read until stop, and a bind with a positive port reports that port. [D-107] Decision: for a bind with port `0` the in-memory adapter assigns a positive port from a counter starting at `49152`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-5]
- [x] AC-6: The SPI declares a blocking stop that stops accepting, waits for in-flight exchanges until a deadline passed by the core and then force-closes the remaining connections, and a separate final release that waits at most a fixed margin for the adapter's threads to end; the in-memory adapter implements both and a test shows an in-flight exchange still running at the deadline is force-closed. [D-107] Decision: an exchange in flight at the deadline is aborted so its client response reports `abnormallyClosed`, and the in-memory adapter's release shuts its worker pool waiting at most 1 second. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-6]

### CON-HTTP-001 — Engine-free placement of the HttpAdapter SPI and the dispatcher

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-spi, engine-free |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall place the `HttpAdapter` SPI and the dispatcher in `NaruServe-api` (main source set) using only JDK and NaruServe types, so that neither references TinyHttpd, Servlet, Gson, `android.*` or Kotlin stdlib classes.

#### Rationale

Covers D-104 and the engine-free placement part of D-394. Checked by the wave 1 class-reference and animal-sniffer API 21 checks over the `NaruServe-api` main source set.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Every type of the `HttpAdapter` SPI is compiled from the `NaruServe-api` main source set, and its signatures reference only `java.*` types (excluding `java.nio.file.*`, `java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture` and `java.net.http.*`), `com.snoworca.naruserve` types and `org.jspecify` annotation types. [D-104]
- [x] AC-2: The dispatcher class is compiled from the `NaruServe-api` main source set and the `:NaruServe-api` class-reference check passes with no reference to `android/`, `javax/servlet/`, `jakarta/servlet/`, Gson, TinyHttpd or `kotlin/` classes. [D-104; D-394]
- [x] AC-3: `./gradlew build` runs the animal-sniffer API 21 check over the `NaruServe-api` main source set containing the SPI and the dispatcher and it passes. [D-104; D-394]

### FR-HTTP-001 — In-memory HttpAdapter and test harness in the test fixtures

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-spi, test-fixtures |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall ship in the `NaruServe-api` test fixtures source set an in-memory `HttpAdapter` that runs requests through the full core pipeline without sockets, and a harness that builds the internal `RestApp` implementation over it, runs the route-table freeze and sends requests, mirroring the TinyHttpd target handling recorded for wave 3.

#### Rationale

Covers D-108, the in-memory side of D-399 and D-442 (I-021), the wave decisions on adapter halves and the working app in wave 3, and the carried TinyHttpd behaviour (authority-form `CONNECT` answered `501` by the adapter, absolute-form target rewritten to its path). The TinyHttpd halves and the adapter value comparison (residual r5 W1-001, W2-003) are wave 5 work.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The in-memory adapter is a class under `NaruServe-api/src/testFixtures/java` implementing `HttpAdapter`, it is packaged in the `NaruServe-api` test-fixtures jar (CON-BUILD-006 AC-8), and `NaruServe-api`'s own tests use it. [D-108]
- [x] AC-2: A request sent through the in-memory adapter passes through the same dispatcher, router, `Request` and `Response` code as an adapter in production, opens no socket, and returns the status, headers and body bytes the core wrote. [D-108]
- [x] AC-3: The harness builds the internal `RestApp` implementation over the in-memory adapter, runs the route-table freeze once and then sends requests, and a registration error raised by the freeze reaches the test caller as the thrown exception. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; D-397] Decision: the harness `start()` rethrows the freeze's `RoutingConfigException` itself, unwrapped. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-4: The in-memory adapter passes the request-target path and query string to the core undecoded, so `GET /a%20b?q=%20` reaches `req.rawPath()` = `/a%20b` and the raw query `q=%20` (`req.query("q")` = ` `). [D-399; wave decision: Adapter halves]
- [x] AC-5: The in-memory adapter answers an authority-form `CONNECT host:443` request itself with `501` without calling the dispatcher, mirroring TinyHttpd. [wave-3 carried: CONNECT authority-form; I-021] Decision: any `CONNECT` whose target does not start with `/` gets `501` with `Content-Length: 0`, and the `ExchangeHandler` is not called. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-6: The in-memory adapter rewrites an absolute-form target such as `http://example.com/a?x=1` to raw path `/a` and raw query `x=1` before dispatch, mirroring TinyHttpd. [wave-3 carried: absolute-form target; I-021] Decision: of an absolute-form `scheme://authority/path?query` target only the path (or `/` when the path is empty) and the query are kept. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-2 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-7: The in-memory adapter passes an asterisk-form `OPTIONS *` target to the core as raw path `*`, as TinyHttpd does. [D-442; I-021; loop D: V1-006/V2-004]

### ~~FR-HTTP-002 — Dispatcher pipeline and pre-request checks before route lookup~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-HTTP-021]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, dispatcher |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide a dispatcher that takes each exchange from an `HttpAdapter`, checks the asterisk-form `OPTIONS *` first, then rejects a raw path not starting with `/` with `400`, then rejects a raw path refused by the wave 2 path validator with `400`, and only then calls the router lookup and either invokes the matched `RouteHandler` with a `Request` and a `Response` or writes the automatic response. The pre-request rejections are answered before any `Request` is built.

#### Rationale

Covers D-394, D-397, the wave decision on order before lookup, the carried rule that the dispatcher runs the path validator before lookup (400, no decoding), and the residual on pre-request rejections (D-422, D-442, D-052, D-117, D-129, I-014). T01, T02, T03, T05 and T06 run their HTTP-level assertions through the in-memory adapter.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Through the harness, a `GET /users/7` request with `app.get("/users/:id", h)` registered reaches `h` once with a `Request` and a `Response`, and the status, headers and body `h` wrote come back through the in-memory adapter. [D-394; D-397]
- [x] AC-2: With a recording handler registered through `app.route(Method.any(), "/", h)` and `app.route(Method.any(), "/:x", h)`, `OPTIONS *` is answered `204` before path validation (so `*` is not rejected with `400`), and a validator rejection happens before the router lookup: `GET /%2e%2e` and `GET /a%2Fb` (both rejected by the `PathValidator`) are answered `400`, no lookup exception escapes, and `h` registered on the matching pattern `/:x` records no call. [wave decision: Order before lookup; D-442; D-052] Decision: the dispatcher checks in this order: `OPTIONS` with raw path `*` gives `204`, any raw path not starting with `/` gives `400`, a `PathValidator` rejection gives `400`, then the router lookup runs. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-3: A raw path that the wave 2 `PathValidator` rejects is answered `400` with `Content-Length: 0` and an empty body without a router lookup, and the validator sees the raw form with a single decoding: `/%2e%2e` and `/a%2Fb` are answered `400`, while `/%252e%252e` reaches a `/:x` route with `req.param("x")` = `%2e%2e`. [wave-3 carried: path validator before lookup; D-052; wave decision: Automatic response bodies]
- [x] AC-4: An unrecognised method token is answered `501`, a raw path not starting with `/` `400`, a validator rejection `400` and `OPTIONS *` `204`, each before a `Request` is built, so no handler sees them; a recording handler registered through `app.route(Method.any(), "/", h)` and `app.route(Method.any(), "/:x", h)` records that it was never called. [wave-3 residual: pre-request rejections; D-422]
- [x] AC-5: In every handler call `req.method()` returns a non-null `Method` and `req.path()` a non-null decoded path. [wave-3 residual: pre-request rejections]
- [x] AC-6: The HTTP-level assertions of T01, T02, T03, T05 and T06 run as tests through the in-memory adapter and the harness in `./gradlew build`. [D-397]

### ~~FR-HTTP-003 — Automatic responses for router lookup results~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-HTTP-022]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, dispatcher, auto-response |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall answer the no-match result with `404`, the method-mismatch result with `405` and an `Allow` header, the automatic-OPTIONS result with `204` and an `Allow` header, the unsupported-method result with `501` and the WebSocket-only result with `426` plus `Upgrade: websocket` and `Connection: Upgrade`, where in wave 3 (until wave 4 gives the error statuses the standard error body) the `400`, `404`, `405`, `426` and `501` answers carry an empty body with `Content-Length: 0`, and the `204` answers carry no body.

#### Rationale

Covers D-395 (T02, T05, T06) and the wave decisions on automatic response bodies and `426` over TinyHttpd (the in-memory tests assert both `426` headers; the TinyHttpd gap is recorded in `carried-into-wave-7.md`, wave 7). Wave 4 gives the error statuses the standard error body.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Through the harness, a request whose path matches no route is answered `404` with `Content-Length: 0` and, in wave 3 (until wave 4 adds the standard error body), an empty body. [D-395; wave decision: Automatic response bodies]
- [x] AC-2: With only `POST /p` registered, `GET /p` is answered `405` with `Allow` listing `POST, OPTIONS`, and with `GET /r` registered `DELETE /r` is answered `405` with `Allow` including the automatic `HEAD` and `OPTIONS`, both with `Content-Length: 0` in wave 3. [D-395; T02] Decision: the `Allow` value joins the allowed methods (or `Method.any()` for `OPTIONS *`) with `, ` in `MethodSet` iteration (`Method` declaration) order. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-3: With `GET /r` and `POST /r` registered and no `OPTIONS` route, `OPTIONS /r` is answered `204` with `Allow` = `GET, HEAD, POST, OPTIONS`, no body and no `Content-Length` header. [D-395; T05; wave decision: Automatic response bodies]
- [x] AC-4: A request with an unsupported method token is answered `501` with `Content-Length: 0` and, in wave 3, an empty body. [D-395]
- [x] AC-5: With a WebSocket-only route registered through the internal hook of FR-HTTP-008 AC-7, a plain `GET` to that path is answered `426` with headers `Upgrade: websocket` and `Connection: Upgrade` and, in wave 3, `Content-Length: 0`, asserted through the in-memory adapter. [D-395; T06; wave decision: 426 over TinyHttpd] Decision: the upgrade flag passed to the lookup is `Router.isWebSocketUpgrade(token, Connection, Upgrade)` with each header's values joined by `, `. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-6: With the same WebSocket-only hook route, a `GET` carrying `Upgrade: websocket` and `Connection: Upgrade` (for which the router returns a `MATCHED` result with route kind `WEBSOCKET`) is answered in wave 3 with the same `426`, both headers and `Content-Length: 0`, and no handshake is attempted, until wave 7 adds `app.ws`; this is the dispatcher mapping that FR-HTTP-008 AC-7 relies on. [D-395; T06; FR-ROUTE-015]
- [x] AC-7: After `app.route(Method.any().except(Method.DELETE), "/t", h)`, `DELETE /t` through the in-memory harness is answered `405` with `Allow` = `GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, OPTIONS` (the router's `Method` declaration order, FR-ROUTE-010 AC-1) and `Content-Length: 0` in wave 3. [D-395] [T02]

### FR-HTTP-004 — Automatic HEAD through the GET handler

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, dispatcher, auto-response |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall, for an automatic-`HEAD` result, run the `GET` handler and send its status and headers without a body, with `req.method()` returning `HEAD` inside that handler.

#### Rationale

Covers D-396 (T05), D-398 (the `req.method()` value) and the wave decision on `req.method()` during automatic `HEAD`; also D-042 (actual method reported).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With only `GET /h` registered writing status `200`, header `X-A: 1` and body `hello`, `HEAD /h` through the harness is answered `200` with `X-A: 1` and no body bytes. [D-396; T05]
- [x] AC-2: Inside the `GET` handler run for an automatic `HEAD`, `req.method()` returns `Method.HEAD`. [D-398; D-042; wave decision: req.method() during automatic HEAD]

### ~~FR-HTTP-005 — Asterisk-form OPTIONS and raw paths not starting with a slash~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-HTTP-023]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, dispatcher, auto-response |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall answer the asterisk-form request `OPTIONS *` (raw path `*`) with `204` and an `Allow` header listing the methods of `Method.any()`, without route lookup or path validation, and answer any other raw path that does not start with `/` with `400`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-442 and the loop D fix V1-006/V2-004 (I-021), on the in-memory adapter in wave 3; the TinyHttpd half of D-442 and I-021 is wave 5 work. D-036 defines `Method.any()`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Through the in-memory adapter, `OPTIONS *` is answered `204` with `Allow` listing exactly the methods of `Method.any()` in `Method` declaration order and no body, even when no route is registered. [D-442; loop D: V1-006/V2-004]
- [x] AC-2: `OPTIONS *` reaches neither the router lookup nor the path validator: with a recording handler registered through `app.route(Method.any(), "/", h)` and `app.route(Method.any(), "/:x", h)`, `OPTIONS *` is still answered `204` with the `Allow` of AC-1 (not the validator's `400` for a raw path `*`), and `h` records no call. [D-442; D-052]
- [x] AC-3: A raw path `abc` and a raw path `*` with method `GET` are each answered `400` with `Content-Length: 0` without route lookup. [D-442; wave decision: Automatic response bodies]
- [x] AC-4: The `OPTIONS *` and non-`/` cases are tested through the in-memory adapter in wave 3; their TinyHttpd counterparts are not asserted in this requirement. [I-021; wave decision: Adapter halves]

### ~~FR-HTTP-006 — Interim handler failure and unfinished-response handling~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-HTTP-024]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, dispatcher, error-handling |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall, until wave 4 adds the error path, answer an exception thrown by a handler with `500` and an empty body while the response is not committed, abort the response when the exception is thrown after commit or while streaming, and finish a non-streaming response left unfinished by a returning handler with the status and headers set so far and an empty body.

#### Rationale

Covers the wave decision on interim failure handling (D-126, D-127, D-129, D-134 replaced in wave 4; D-097, D-099, D-131).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handler that throws `RuntimeException` before writing anything is answered `500` with an empty body through the harness. [wave decision: Interim failure handling]
- [x] AC-2: A handler that throws a checked `IOException` after setting status `201` but before commit is answered `500` with an empty body. [wave decision: Interim failure handling] Decision: the status and headers the handler set are dropped and the `500` carries only `Content-Length: 0`; the exception is not rethrown to the adapter. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-3: A handler that throws after commit while the response is `STREAMING` (the only committed-but-incomplete state a wave 3 handler can reach) leaves the response `ABORTED` and the in-memory adapter reports the exchange closed abnormally instead of a `500`; a handler that throws after the response is `COMPLETED` (for example after `res.send("x")`) leaves it `COMPLETED`, because abort is a no-op there, with the sent bytes unchanged and no `500`. [D-097; D-099; wave decision: abort() after completion; wave decision: Interim failure handling]
- [x] AC-4: A handler that sets `status(202)` and `header("X-A", "1")` and returns without a body write or `end()` yields `202` with `X-A: 1` and an empty body, and the response is `COMPLETED`. [wave decision: Interim failure handling] Decision: when the handler returns with the response `NEW` the dispatcher calls `end()`, and with it `STREAMING` it runs the pending stream copy on the same adapter thread. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-10]

### IR-HTTP-005 — RestApp route registration and routing policy interface

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-surface, routing |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide a public `RestApp` interface in `com.snoworca.naruserve` whose wave 3 methods are the route registration methods `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `route(Method, ...)`, `route(MethodSet, ...)` and `route(path)`, and the routing policy method `routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)`, with no `serve(...)` method.

#### Rationale

Covers D-040, D-041, D-046, D-393 and D-398 (routing policy API name), the wave decision on remaining markers (`app.routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)`), and the carried item that the routing-policy configuration API names are decided in wave 3. Later waves add their own `RestApp` methods.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `RestApp` declares `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head` and `options`, each taking `(String path, RouteHandler handler)` and returning `RestApp`. [D-040]
- [x] AC-2: `RestApp` declares `route(Method, String, RouteHandler)` and `route(MethodSet, String, RouteHandler)` returning `RestApp`, and `route(String)` returning `RouteBuilder`. [D-041; D-043]
- [x] AC-3: `RestApp` declares `routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)` returning `RestApp`, taking the existing `RouterPolicy` built with `RouterPolicy.builder()` (`mergeSlashes`, `ignoreTrailingSlash`, `caseInsensitive`, `enableTrace`, `enableConnect`). [D-393; D-398; wave decision: Remaining markers; wave-3 carried: routing-policy API names]
- [x] AC-4: A reflection test finds no method named `serve` on `RestApp`. [D-046]
- [x] AC-5: At the wave 3 merge, a reflection test finds that the methods of the public `RestApp` interface are exactly the route registration and routing policy methods above (no `ws`, `use` or `listen` until their waves add them; the internal WebSocket hook of FR-HTTP-008 is not on the interface); nullness follows the wave 1 convention: the package is `@NullMarked` (non-null by default, which counts as `@NonNull`) and any nullable reference-type parameter or return carries `@Nullable`, which the test checks through the package annotation and the `@Nullable` positions listed in `docs/api/app.md`. [D-393; D-072]
- [x] AC-6: Kotlin source `app.get("/x") { req, res -> res.send("ok") }` compiles and returns `RestApp`. [D-040]

### IR-HTTP-006 — RouteBuilder per-method registration and duplicate equivalence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, http-surface, routing |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `app.route(path)` return a public `RouteBuilder` whose `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head` and `options` each register a separate handler on that path, with `app.get(path, h)` and `app.route(path).get(h)` producing the same registration so that registering both for one path is a duplicate.

#### Rationale

Covers D-043 and D-044 (T03) and the wave decision on the working app in wave 3 (the duplicate is raised by the freeze and asserted through the harness).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `RouteBuilder` is a public interface in `com.snoworca.naruserve` declaring `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head` and `options`, each taking a `RouteHandler` and returning `RouteBuilder`, so `app.route("/u/:id").get(h1).put(h2).delete(h3)` compiles. [D-043] Decision: each `RouteBuilder` registration method returns the same builder instance it was called on. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 SDS-AC-7]
- [x] AC-2: Through the harness, after `app.route("/u/:id").get(h1).put(h2).delete(h3)`, `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE /u/1` each reach only their own handler. [D-043; T03]
- [x] AC-3: Registering `app.get("/x", h1)` and `app.route("/x").get(h2)` makes the harness freeze throw `RoutingConfigException` naming the duplicate. [D-044; T03; wave decision: Working app in wave 3]
- [x] AC-4: `app.get("/x", h)` and `app.route("/x").get(h)` record the same method set, pattern and route kind in the router registration. [D-044]

### FR-HTTP-007 — One handler for a Method or MethodSet reports the actual method

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, routing, dispatcher |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/2 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall register the one handler given to `route(Method, path, handler)` or `route(MethodSet, path, handler)` for every method in the argument, and report the actual request method through `req.method()` when a request reaches that handler.

#### Rationale

Covers D-041 and D-042 (T01) and the wave decision on `req.method()` during automatic `HEAD`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: After `app.route(Method.get().post().delete(), "/users/:id", h)`, `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE /users/1` through the harness all reach `h`, and `PUT /users/1` is answered `405`. [D-041; T01] Decision: `app.get(p, h)` and a builder `get(h)` record the same registration (method set `MethodSet.of(GET)`, pattern `p`, HTTP route kind), so the freeze reports such a pair as a duplicate. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-2: Inside `h`, `req.method()` returns `Method.GET`, `Method.POST` and `Method.DELETE` for those three requests respectively. [D-042; T01]
- [x] AC-3: After `app.route(Method.PATCH, "/p", h)`, `PATCH /p` reaches `h` with `req.method()` = `Method.PATCH`. [D-041; D-042]
- [x] AC-4: `HEAD /users/1` reaches `h` through automatic `HEAD` with `req.method()` = `Method.HEAD`. [D-042; wave decision: req.method() during automatic HEAD]

### FR-HTTP-008 — Internal RestApp implementation with deferred router build and freeze

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, routing, app |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/3 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide in an internal package of `NaruServe-api` a `RestApp` implementation that holds the router and the dispatcher, parses each registered pattern at the registration call, records the registrations and the routing policy, builds the `Router` with that policy only at the route-table freeze, and rejects any registration or `routerPolicy` call after the freeze with `IllegalStateException`. Wave 3 adds no public factory for it.

#### Rationale

Covers D-393 and D-044 (duplicate raised at freeze) and the wave decisions on the working app in wave 3 (D-047, D-048, D-058, D-111, D-154) and settings after the freeze (D-114, D-152). Wave 4 makes `listen()` run the same freeze and ties the after-freeze rule to the `NEW` state.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The implementation is a public class in a package documented as internal and not part of the API contract, it implements `RestApp`, and it holds the router and the dispatcher. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; D-393] Decision: the implementation is public class `DefaultRestApp` in package `com.snoworca.naruserve.internal`, whose package javadoc states it is not API. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-14]
- [x] AC-2: A registration with an invalid pattern (one that `RoutePattern.parse` rejects) throws at the registration call itself, before any freeze. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; D-047; D-048]
- [x] AC-3: `app.get("/a/b", h)` followed by `app.routerPolicy(RouterPolicy.builder().mergeSlashes(true).build())` makes `GET /a//b` reach `h` after the harness freeze, showing the policy applies to registrations made before it. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; FR-ROUTE-006]
- [x] AC-4: The `Router` is constructed only at the freeze, and a duplicate of D-044 surfaces as the freeze's `RoutingConfigException`, not at the second registration call. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; D-044] Decision: the freeze propagates the `RoutingConfigException` unchanged and leaves the app unfrozen, so `dispatcher()` keeps throwing `IllegalStateException`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-5: After the harness freeze, `app.get(...)`, `app.route(Method, path, h)`, `app.route(MethodSet, path, h)` and `app.routerPolicy(...)` each throw `IllegalStateException`, and `RouteBuilder` registration methods (`get`, `put`, ...) throw `IllegalStateException` too, including on a builder obtained from `app.route(path)` before the freeze. [wave decision: Settings after the freeze] Decision: the internal WebSocket-route hook also throws `IllegalStateException` after the freeze, and a refused call records nothing. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-3 SDS-AC-13]
- [x] AC-6: No public static method or constructor in package `com.snoworca.naruserve` returns a `RestApp` in wave 3. [wave decision: Working app in wave 3; D-111]
- [x] AC-7: The internal implementation (not the public `RestApp` interface) offers an internal WebSocket-route registration hook that records a WebSocket registration and passes it to `Router.addWebSocket` at the freeze; the test-fixtures harness uses it to register a WebSocket-only route, and wave 3 dispatches no handshake to that route (a matching upgrade request gets the same `426` as a plain `GET`) until wave 7 adds `app.ws`. [D-395; T06; wave decision: Working app in wave 3]

### FR-HTTP-009 — Request path, raw path and path parameter accessors

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `req.path()` return the decoded path without the query string, `req.rawPath()` return the raw, undecoded path without the query string, and `req.param(name)` return the decoded value of a variable of the matched pattern and throw an exception for a name absent from that pattern.

#### Rationale

Covers D-073, D-074, D-075 and the wave decision on remaining markers (`rawPath()` excludes the query string, which stays available through `req.query(...)`).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: For `GET /files/a%20b?x=1` matched by `/files/:name`, `req.path()` returns `/files/a b`. [D-073] Decision: `req.path()` percent-decodes the raw path once as UTF-8, keeps `+` as `+`, and applies no `mergeSlashes`, trailing-slash or case policy. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-1] Under `mergeSlashes(true)`, `GET /a//b` matched by `/a/b` gives `req.path()` = `/a//b` (the policy affects only route matching, not the decoded path). [wave 3 implementation decision]
- [x] AC-2: For the same request `req.rawPath()` returns `/files/a%20b`, without `?x=1`. [D-074; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-3: For the same request `req.param("name")` returns `a b`. [D-075]
- [x] AC-4: `req.param("missing")` for a name not in the matched pattern throws an exception instead of returning `null`. [D-075] Decision: the exception is `IllegalArgumentException` and its message contains the name. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-2]

### ~~FR-HTTP-010 — Query accessors with form-urlencoded decoding and malformed-query 400~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-HTTP-025]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request, query |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall decode the raw query string as UTF-8 `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` text with `+` read as a space, make `req.query(name)` return the first value or `null` and `req.queries(name)` every value in request order or an empty list, and answer a query with malformed percent-encoding or invalid UTF-8 with `400` before the handler runs.

#### Rationale

Covers D-076, D-077, D-078 (T07, in-memory part), the wave decision on interim failure handling (query decoded before the handler) and the residual on pre-request rejections (a malformed query is decoded after the `Request` exists, so D-117 and D-129 apply to it).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: For `?a=1&a=2&b=3`, `req.query("a")` returns `1`, `req.queries("a")` returns `[1, 2]`, `req.query("c")` returns `null` and `req.queries("c")` returns an empty list. [D-076; T07]
- [x] AC-2: For `?q=a+b%20c&%ED%95%9C=%EA%B8%80`, `req.query("q")` returns `a b c` and `req.query("한")` returns `글`. [D-077; T07] Decision: a pair without `=` has the empty string as its value, empty names are kept, and names and values decode `+` as space. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-3: A query `?x=%zz`, a query `?x=%E0%A4` (truncated UTF-8) and a query `?x=%C3%28` (invalid UTF-8) are each answered `400` and the matched handler is not called. [D-078; T07; wave decision: Interim failure handling] Decision: a malformed `%XX` or bytes that are not valid UTF-8 (decoder `CodingErrorAction.REPORT`) make the query parse throw `IllegalArgumentException`, which the dispatcher answers `400` with `Content-Length: 0` before the handler runs. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-4]

### FR-HTTP-011 — Header, remote address, secure flag and Last-Event-ID accessors

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request, headers |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `req.header(name)` match names case-insensitively and return the first value or `null`, `req.headers(name)` return every value or an empty list, `req.remoteAddress()` and `req.isSecure()` report the adapter's values while ignoring `X-Forwarded-*` and `Forwarded` headers, and `req.lastEventId()` return the `Last-Event-ID` header value or `null`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-079 (T07), D-080 and D-081. The proxy-trust configuration API of D-080 belongs to #8 and does not exist in wave 3, so proxy headers are always ignored here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With headers `X-Tag: a` and `x-tag: b`, `req.header("X-TAG")` returns `a`, `req.headers("x-Tag")` returns `[a, b]`, `req.header("None")` returns `null` and `req.headers("None")` returns an empty list. [D-079; T07] Decision: the empty list returned for no match is unmodifiable. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-5]
- [x] AC-2: With the in-memory adapter reporting remote address `10.0.0.5` and secure `false`, a request carrying `X-Forwarded-For: 1.2.3.4`, `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` and `Forwarded: for=1.2.3.4;proto=https` still yields `req.remoteAddress()` = `10.0.0.5` and `req.isSecure()` = `false`. [D-080]
- [x] AC-3: `req.lastEventId()` returns `42` for header `Last-Event-ID: 42` and `null` when the header is absent. [D-081] Decision: when `Last-Event-ID` appears more than once the first value is returned. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-6]

### FR-HTTP-012 — Per-request concurrent attribute store

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request, attributes |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall store request attributes per request in a concurrent map that never holds `null`, where `req.attribute(key, value)` stores a value or removes the key when the value is `null`, and `req.attribute(key, type)` returns the value, `null` for a missing key, or throws `ClassCastException` when the stored value is not of `type`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-082, D-083 and the wave decisions on null attribute values and remaining markers (`ClassCastException` on a type mismatch). D-072 makes the value parameter `@Nullable`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: After `req.attribute("user", u)`, `req.attribute("user", User.class)` returns `u`, and `req.attribute("none", User.class)` returns `null`. [D-082]
- [x] AC-2: An attribute stored while handling one request is not visible through `attribute(key, type)` of a second request, including two requests handled concurrently. [D-082]
- [x] AC-3: Eight threads writing distinct keys to the same request's attributes concurrently leave every key readable with its value and no exception. [D-083]
- [x] AC-4: With a typed `null` variable `Object v = null`, `req.attribute("k", v)` removes `k`, so a later `req.attribute("k", Object.class)` returns `null`; the value parameter is annotated `@Nullable`. [wave decision: Null attribute values; D-072]
- [x] AC-5: `req.attribute("k", Integer.class)` with a stored `String` throws `ClassCastException`. [wave decision: Remaining markers]

### FR-HTTP-013 — Response chaining and default status

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `res.status` and `res.header` return the same `Response` for chaining, a rule that `res.addCookie` and `res.cache` follow once waves 5 and 6 add them, and default a response to status `200`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-089 and D-090 and the wave decision on the request and response surface (`addCookie` and `cache` are not added in wave 3).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `res.status(201)` and `res.header("X-A", "1")` each return the same `Response` instance they were called on, so `res.status(201).header("X-A", "1").send("ok")` yields `201` with `X-A: 1`. [D-089] Decision: `header` with a name already present in another case replaces that entry, keeping one header of the name, as Express `res.set` does. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-1] `res.status(code)` throws `IllegalArgumentException` for a code outside 100–999 (Express 5 parity), and this range check runs before the response-state check. [wave 3 implementation decision]
- [x] AC-2: A handler that calls only `res.send("ok")` yields status `200`. [D-090]

### FR-HTTP-014 — Response body writers send, html, stream and end

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response, body |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `res.send(String)` write UTF-8 text with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8` unless a `Content-Type` was set, `res.html(String)` write UTF-8 text with `Content-Type: text/html; charset=UTF-8`, `res.send(byte[])` write the bytes unchanged with `application/octet-stream` when no `Content-Type` was set, `res.stream(InputStream, contentType)` copy the stream to its end in bounded chunks while taking ownership of the stream and closing it when the transfer ends by success, failure or abort, and `res.end()` complete the response without a body.

#### Rationale

Covers D-091, D-092, D-093, D-094, D-095 and the wave decisions on `stream()` closing (W2-007) and remaining markers (`application/octet-stream` default, as Express does for a `Buffer`). No chunk size is fixed here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `res.send("hé")` writes the UTF-8 bytes `68 C3 A9` with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8`, and after `res.header("Content-Type", "text/csv")` it keeps `text/csv`. [D-091] Decision: a body writer adds its default `Content-Type` only when no `Content-Type` header is set, compared case-insensitively. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-2: `res.html("<p>é</p>")` writes UTF-8 bytes with `Content-Type: text/html; charset=UTF-8`. [D-092]
- [x] AC-3: `res.send(new byte[]{0, (byte) 0xFF, 10})` writes exactly those three bytes with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` when none was set, and keeps an explicitly set `Content-Type`. [D-093; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-4: `res.stream(in, "application/pdf")` with a 1 MiB input writes all bytes in order with `Content-Type: application/pdf`, and a counting input stream shows that every `read(byte[], int, int)` call requests fewer bytes than the 1 MiB input. [D-094] Decision: the copy reads with an 8192-byte buffer and writes each chunk to the exchange output stream in order. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-5: `res.stream(...)` closes the input stream exactly once after a successful copy, after a read failure, and after `res.abort(...)` during the transfer, including when the handler already returned; the caller does not close it. [D-094; wave decision: stream() closing] Decision: the single close is guarded by a compare-and-set. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-6: `res.end()` completes the response with the status and headers set so far and no body. [D-095] Decision: on a non-bodyless response `end()` commits with `Content-Length: 0`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-6]
- [x] AC-7: `res.stream(InputStream, contentType)` does not block until the copy ends: it commits the response and returns, the copy to the end of the stream runs after the handler returns, and the response is `STREAMING` until the copy ends; a test whose handler returns right after `res.stream(...)` observes the call return before the input is read to its end. [D-094; D-103; wave decision: stream() closing] Decision: `stream` sets `Content-Type` to the argument, commits with unknown length, enters `STREAMING` and returns; the dispatcher runs the copy on the adapter thread after the handler returns. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-5]

### FR-HTTP-015 — Response commit flag, abort and header CR/LF rejection

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response, state-machine |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make `res.isCommitted()` return `true` once the status line and headers were handed to the transport, make `res.abort(error)` end the response abnormally by closing the connection and set the state to `ABORTED`, and make `res.header(name, value)` reject a name or value containing CR or LF with an exception.

#### Rationale

Covers D-096, D-097, D-098 and the wave decision on `abort()` after completion (no-op on a complete or aborted response).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `res.isCommitted()` is `false` before any body write and `true` after `res.send("x")` handed the status and headers to the in-memory adapter. [D-096] Decision: `abort` before any commit leaves `isCommitted()` `false`, sets `ABORTED` and aborts the exchange without writing a status. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-2: `res.abort(new IOException("x"))` during a `stream(...)` transfer makes the in-memory adapter report the connection closed abnormally and leaves the response state `ABORTED`. [D-097]
- [x] AC-3: `res.abort(...)` on a response that is already `COMPLETED` or `ABORTED` does nothing: no exception, the state is unchanged and the adapter sees no further close. [wave decision: abort() after completion]
- [x] AC-4: `res.header("X\r\nY", "1")`, `res.header("X", "a\nb")` and `res.header("X", "a\rb")` each throw an exception and add no header. [D-098] Decision: the exception is `IllegalArgumentException`, the header list stays unchanged, and the response-state check runs first. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-7] Besides CR and LF, `res.header` also throws `IllegalArgumentException` for a name that is not an RFC 9110 token (empty, or a char outside tchar) and for a value holding any control character other than HTAB (such as NUL or DEL) or any char above U+00FF. [wave 3 implementation decision]

### FR-HTTP-016 — Response state machine with single commit

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response, state-machine |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall move each response through the states `NEW`, `COMMITTED`, `STREAMING`, `COMPLETED` and `ABORTED`, committing at most once, throw an exception for `status` or `header` after commit and for a second body after a body was sent, and keep a streaming response in `STREAMING` after the handler returns until the transfer ends.

#### Rationale

Covers D-099, D-100, D-101 and D-103 and the wave decision on `stream()` closing. D-100 applies to `addCookie` and `cache` once waves 5 and 6 add them; among the streaming responses only `stream` exists in wave 3.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A response starts in `NEW`, is `COMMITTED` once status and headers are handed to the adapter, and reaches `COMPLETED` after `send`; the adapter receives the status line and headers exactly once. [D-099]
- [x] AC-2: `res.status(500)` or `res.header("X", "1")` after `res.send("a")` committed the response throws an exception and the sent status and headers stay unchanged. [D-100] Decision: the exception is `IllegalStateException` naming the current state, thrown in `COMMITTED`, `STREAMING`, `COMPLETED` or `ABORTED`, and nothing changes. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-3: A second body call (`send`, `html`, `stream` or `end`) after a body was sent throws an exception and writes no further bytes. [D-101]
- [x] AC-4: After `res.stream(...)` returns, the response is `STREAMING` until the copy ends, then `COMPLETED`, or `ABORTED` on abort; a read failure of the input stream during the copy, including after the handler returned, ends the response `ABORTED` with the input stream closed (FR-HTTP-014 AC-5). [D-103; D-097; wave decision: stream() closing] Decision: a read failure during the copy sets `ABORTED`, calls `exchange.abort()` and closes the input, without rethrowing to the adapter. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-5: While the response is `STREAMING` (committed but not completed), `res.status(500)` and `res.header("X", "1")` each throw an exception and the status and headers the adapter received stay unchanged. [D-100; D-099]

### FR-HTTP-017 — Bodyless 204, 304 and HEAD responses

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response, body |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall send responses with status `204` or `304`, and every response to a `HEAD` request, without a message body, discarding any body the handler supplied.

#### Rationale

Covers D-102 and the wave decision on automatic response bodies (`204` replies carry no body). Used by automatic `HEAD` (D-396).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handler that calls `res.status(204).send("x")` yields `204` with zero body bytes through the in-memory adapter. [D-102] Decision: a `204` or `304` response commits with length 0, adds no `Content-Length` header and drops the body bytes. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-2: A handler that calls `res.status(304).send(new byte[]{1, 2})` yields `304` with zero body bytes. [D-102]
- [x] AC-3: An explicit `HEAD /h` route whose handler calls `res.send("body")` yields its status and headers with zero body bytes. [D-102] Decision: a `HEAD` response sends the `Content-Length` the body would have had and writes zero body bytes; a `HEAD` `stream` closes the input without copying. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-5 SDS-AC-13]
- [x] AC-4: The automatic `204` answers of `OPTIONS` and `OPTIONS *` carry zero body bytes. [D-102; wave decision: Automatic response bodies]

### FR-HTTP-018 — Mutation after completion throws while read accessors stay usable

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, response, state-machine |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall throw `IllegalStateException` for a mutating `Response` call (`status`, `header` or a body write) after the response completed, a rule that `addCookie` and `cache` follow from waves 5 and 6, while the read accessors of `Request` and `Response`, `isCommitted()` and `abort()` stay usable after completion.

#### Rationale

Covers D-084 and the wave decisions on the request and response surface (`addCookie` and `cache` from waves 5 and 6) and `abort()` after completion. Middleware and `onError` arrive in wave 4; wave 3 checks the rule from code that runs after the handler finished the response.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: After `res.send("a")` completed the response, `res.status(201)`, `res.header("X", "1")`, `res.send("b")`, `res.html("c")`, `res.send(new byte[0])`, `res.stream(in, "text/plain")` and `res.end()` each throw `IllegalStateException`. [D-084]
- [x] AC-2: After completion, `req.path()`, `req.query("q")`, `req.header("H")`, `req.attribute("k", Object.class)` and `res.isCommitted()` return their values without exception. [D-084]
- [x] AC-3: After completion, `res.abort(...)` returns without exception and changes nothing. [D-084; wave decision: abort() after completion]

### FR-HTTP-019 — Request body ownership with BodyState and BodyAlreadyConsumedException

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request, body |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall track each request body with an internal `BodyState` moving from `UNREAD` through `PARSING` to `CONSUMED` or `CLOSED`, throw the public unchecked `BodyAlreadyConsumedException` of package `com.snoworca.naruserve` for any body read after the first one, and let repeated `form`, `file`, `files` and `parts` reads share one parse as the only exemption. Wave 3 exposes no public body-reading method.

#### Rationale

Covers D-085, D-086, D-087 and the wave decision on body ownership (W2-004; I-012). The JSON, form, multipart and raw readers arrive in waves 5 and 6, so wave 3 tests these rules through `BodyState` directly; the raw kind is an internal consumer of the body stream.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `BodyAlreadyConsumedException` is a public class in `com.snoworca.naruserve` extending `RuntimeException`. [D-086; wave decision: Body ownership]
- [x] AC-2: `BodyState` is an internal class whose state is `UNREAD` initially, `PARSING` while a reader holds the body, and `CONSUMED` after a completed read or `CLOSED` after the body was closed; a unit test drives each transition. [D-085] Decision: `close` moves to `CLOSED` from any state, and a later `begin` or shared parse throws `BodyAlreadyConsumedException`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-3: A unit test acquiring the body through `BodyState` after a first read gets `BodyAlreadyConsumedException` when the second read is of a different kind (JSON, form/multipart or raw) or is a second JSON or raw read; the shared form/multipart parse of AC-4 is the only exemption. [D-086] Decision: `begin` in any state other than `UNREAD` throws `BodyAlreadyConsumedException` naming the current state. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-4: A unit test acquiring the shared form/multipart parse through `BodyState` repeatedly gets the same parse result each time without a second read of the stream and without `BodyAlreadyConsumedException`. [D-087] Decision: the shared parse after `begin`, or `begin` after the shared parse, throws `BodyAlreadyConsumedException`; a parser failure leaves `CONSUMED` with no cached result and later calls throw `BodyAlreadyConsumedException`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-5: A reflection test finds no public body-reading method on `Request` at the wave 3 merge (wave 5 adds the body readers). [wave decision: Body ownership]

### FR-HTTP-020 — Limit-enforcing request body input stream

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, request, body, limits |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide an internal limit-enforcing input stream that requires a positive limit at construction and fails a read as soon as the bytes read from the incoming stream exceed that limit, whether or not the request carries `Content-Length`, so body size limits are enforced before and during parsing.

#### Rationale

Covers D-088 and the wave decision on body ownership (positive limit required, no unlimited default; I-012). The HTTP-level `413` checks land with the body readers in waves 5 and 6; wave 3 tests the stream directly. No default limit value is set here.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The stream is constructed from the source stream, a limit and an optional declared `Content-Length`; constructing it with limit `0` or a negative limit throws `IllegalArgumentException`. [D-088; wave decision: Body ownership]
- [x] AC-2: With limit `10`, reading a 10-byte source returns all 10 bytes and end of stream without error. [D-088]
- [x] AC-3: With limit `10`, reading an 11-byte source without a declared `Content-Length` (a chunked-style source of unknown length) throws the internal over-limit `IOException` subclass (a type distinct from plain `IOException`, named by the implementer) on the read that would return byte 11, and no byte beyond the limit is returned. [D-088] Decision: the over-limit type is `BodyTooLargeException`, and the stream reads at most limit+1 bytes from the source. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-4: With limit `10` and a declared `Content-Length` of `11`, the first read throws the same internal over-limit `IOException` subclass without reading any byte from the source, so the waves that add body readers can tell it from a transport `IOException` and answer `413`. [D-088] Decision: the exception is `BodyTooLargeException`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-4 SDS-AC-13]

### ~~OPS-HTTP-001 — Wave 3 decisions recorded in docs/api and their markers removed~~ [DISCARDED → see OPS-HTTP-003]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, docs |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall record the wave 3 decisions in `docs/api/request.md`, `docs/api/response.md`, `docs/api/routing.md`, `docs/api/app.md`, `docs/api/defaults.md` and `docs/api/express-migration.md`, removing the (미확정) markers on `rawPath()`, the `send(byte[])` default `Content-Type`, `stream()` closing, the attribute type-mismatch exception, the routing-policy setting API, the `req.method()` value during automatic `HEAD` and `express.raw()`/`express.text()`, while keeping the markers owned by other issues.

#### Rationale

Covers D-109, D-398 and the wave decisions on remaining markers, `req.method()` during automatic `HEAD`, `stream()` closing, null attribute values, body ownership (express-migration raw/text line) and docs examples (I-009). The `docs/api/routing.md` marker-count assertion is OPS-ROUTE-002 AC-3 (residual: marker test of wave 2), which this requirement depends on instead of restating; tests read the docs files as `RoutingDocsTest` does.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `docs/api/request.md` states that `rawPath()` returns the raw, undecoded path without the query string (available through `req.query(...)`), gives its Express counterpart as `req.path` before decoding (not `req.originalUrl`), and has no (미확정) marker on the `rawPath()` row. [D-109; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-2: `docs/api/request.md` states that `req.attribute(name, type)` throws `ClassCastException` for a stored value of another type, without a marker, and that a value is removed by storing a typed `null` variable because a literal `null` resolves to the read overload. [D-109; wave decision: Remaining markers; wave decision: Null attribute values]
- [x] AC-3: `docs/api/response.md` states that `send(byte[])` without a `Content-Type` uses `application/octet-stream` and that `stream(InputStream, contentType)` takes ownership and closes the stream on success, failure or abort, with an example that does not use try-with-resources, and the marker line on these two points is gone. [D-109; wave decision: stream() closing; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-4: `docs/api/routing.md` documents `app.routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)` with `RouterPolicy.builder()` (`mergeSlashes`, `ignoreTrailingSlash`, `caseInsensitive`, `enableTrace`, `enableConnect`) as allowed only while the app is in `NEW` (before `listen()`), like every setting in `app.md` (in wave 3 the internal implementation rejects a call after the harness freeze with `IllegalStateException`, FR-HTTP-008), replaces the sentence of section '라우팅 규칙 (wave 2 결정)' saying the policy setting API name is not decided with a pointer to `app.routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)` so that no sentence calls that name undecided, and states that `req.method()` returns `HEAD` during automatic `HEAD`; its marker set is checked by OPS-ROUTE-002 AC-3. [D-398; wave decision: Remaining markers; wave decision: req.method() during automatic HEAD; wave-3 residual: marker test of wave 2]
- [x] AC-5: `docs/api/app.md` lists `routerPolicy(RouterPolicy)` among the `RestApp` methods with a Java and a Kotlin example and adds no (미확정) marker for it. [D-398; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-6: `docs/api/defaults.md` names `app.routerPolicy(...)` with `enableTrace`/`enableConnect` as the explicit `TRACE`/`CONNECT` policy, and that row has no (미확정) marker. [D-398; wave decision: Remaining markers]
- [x] AC-7: `docs/api/express-migration.md` no longer maps `req.originalUrl` to `req.rawPath()`: the `rawPath()` row names `req.path` before decoding as its Express counterpart without a marker, and a kept `req.originalUrl` row points to `req.rawPath()` plus the query string read through `req.query(...)` or states that it has no direct counterpart, without a marker; the `express.raw()`/`express.text()` marker is replaced with the statement that v1 has no raw or text body reader, and the raw/text bullet is removed from the undecided list (section 'Express에 있지만 아직 정해지지 않은 기능'), so the file states once, without contradiction, that v1 has no raw or text body reader. [D-109; wave decision: Remaining markers; wave decision: Body ownership] Decision: the kept `req.originalUrl` row points to `req.rawPath()` plus the query read through `req.query(...)`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-6 SDS-AC-5]
- [x] AC-8: Markers other than those this requirement removes stay, including `file` disposition and `redirect` in `response.md`, proxy trust, `parts()` and `jsonTree()`/`jsonMap()` in `request.md`, `RouteBuilder.use` in `routing.md`, and the markers of `express-migration.md` owned by other issues. [wave decision: Remaining markers]

### ~~OPS-HTTP-002 — docs/api Java examples compile against the wave 3 API~~ [DISCARDED → see OPS-HTTP-004]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-3, feasibility:high, docs, docs-examples |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/4 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall compile, in the root test suite, every ```java block of `docs/api/*.md` whose methods and types exist at the wave 3 merge, against the `NaruServe-api` main classes, skipping only blocks on an explicit, non-stale skip list (I-009).

#### Rationale

Split from OPS-HTTP-001 AC-9 after the wave-3 feasibility check. Covers I-009 and the wave decision on docs examples.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The root `build.gradle` puts the `NaruServe-api` main classes on the root test suite's test classpath (for example `testImplementation project(':NaruServe-api')`) while keeping the wave 1 root test conventions (JUnit 4, `gradleTestKit()`, the existing `test` task dependencies and system properties). [I-009; wave decision: docs examples]
- [x] AC-2: The root test `src/test/java/com/snoworca/naruserve/docs/DocsExamplesCompileTest.java` extracts every ```java fenced block of every `docs/api/*.md` file. [I-009; wave decision: docs examples]
- [x] AC-3: The test compiles a block that declares a top-level type as its own compilation unit and places any other block in a method template that declares `throws Exception`, both with the fixed imports `com.snoworca.naruserve.*`, `com.snoworca.naruserve.routing.*`, `java.util.*` and `java.io.*`, where the template has `app` (`RestApp`), `req` (`Request`) and `res` (`Response`) as fields in scope and declares the stub handlers and stub values the docs name (such as `getUser`, `id` or `user`); compilation runs in process through `javax.tools.ToolProvider.getSystemJavaCompiler()` with the test runtime classpath and `--release 8`, and a compilation error or a missing system compiler fails the test. [I-009; wave decision: docs examples] Decision: a non-type block is wrapped in `class DocExampleN` with fields `app`, `req` and `res` and stub `RouteHandler` fields for the handler names the docs use, inside `void example() throws Exception`; a failure names each block's file, ordinal and diagnostics. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-6 SDS-AC-7] [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-6 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-4: A block is skipped only when it is on an explicit list kept in the test whose entry names the file, the block (its ordinal among the file's ```java blocks or its first line) and one reason: a call to a later-wave method (named), an illustrative copy of an existing type declaration (such as `Method` or `RouteHandler`), or a type of a later wave or of a library absent from the root test classpath (named, such as Gson `@SerializedName`, `Logger`, `JsonCodec`, `UploadFile` or `Middleware`). [I-009; wave decision: docs examples] Decision: the block selector is an ordinal such as `#3` or the block's first line. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-6 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-5: A ```java fence in `docs/api` that holds JavaScript is relabelled ```js. [I-009; wave decision: docs examples]
- [x] AC-6: The test fails when a skip-list entry no longer matches any block of its file, so no stale skip remains. [I-009; wave decision: docs examples]
- [x] AC-7: The blocks of `docs/api/response.md` and `docs/api/routing.md` that call only wave 3 methods are compiled, not skipped: the test asserts that at least one block of each of these two files was compiled. [I-009; wave decision: docs examples]

### CON-HTTP-002 — Public API types avoid Optional, java.util.function, streams and java.nio.file [DRAFT — pending decision]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | wave-3 |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | draft |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall keep every public type in package `com.snoworca.naruserve` free of `java.util.Optional`, `java.util.function`, `java.util.stream` and `java.nio.file` types in its signatures, because the animal-sniffer API 21 check and Android 5.0 users reject them.

#### Rationale

Durable rule of SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-3-1 (public HTTP types), moved to the SRS at SDS close.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: A reflection or class-reference check over the public types of `com.snoworca.naruserve` finds no signature referencing `java.util.Optional`, `java.util.function.*`, `java.util.stream.*` or `java.nio.file.*`, and `./gradlew build` with the animal-sniffer `android-api-level-21` check passes.
